tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#about-matrix-strategies">About matrix strategies<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>A matrix strategy lets you use variables in a single job definition to automatically create multiple job runs that are based on the combinations of the variables. For example, you can use a matrix strategy to test your code in multiple versions of a language or on multiple operating systems.</p>
<h2 id="adding-a-matrix-strategy-to-your-workflow-job" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#adding-a-matrix-strategy-to-your-workflow-job">Adding a matrix strategy to your workflow job<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>Use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.matrix</code> to define a matrix of different job configurations. Within your matrix, define one or more variables followed by an array of values. For example, the following matrix has a variable called <code>version</code> with the value <code>[10, 12, 14]</code> and a variable called <code>os</code> with the value <code>[ubuntu-latest, windows-latest]</code>:</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">jobs:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">example_matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> [<span class="hljs-number">10</span>, <span class="hljs-number">12</span>, <span class="hljs-number">14</span>]
        <span class="hljs-attr">os:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">ubuntu-latest</span>, <span class="hljs-string">windows-latest</span>]
</code></pre>
<p>A job will run for each possible combination of the variables. In this example, the workflow will run six jobs, one for each combination of the <code>os</code> and <code>version</code> variables.</p>
<p>The above matrix will create the jobs in the following order.</p>
<ul>
<li><code>{version: 10, os: ubuntu-latest}</code></li>
<li><code>{version: 10, os: windows-latest}</code></li>
<li><code>{version: 12, os: ubuntu-latest}</code></li>
<li><code>{version: 12, os: windows-latest}</code></li>
<li><code>{version: 14, os: ubuntu-latest}</code></li>
<li><code>{version: 14, os: windows-latest}</code></li>
</ul>
<p>For reference information and examples, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrix">Workflow syntax for GitHub Actions</a>.</p>
<h2 id="using-contexts-to-create-matrices" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#using-contexts-to-create-matrices">Using contexts to create matrices<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>To create matrices with information about workflow runs, variables, runner environments, jobs, and steps, access contexts using the <code>${{ &lt;context&gt; }}</code> expression syntax. For more information about contexts, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts">Contexts reference</a>.</p>
<p>For example, the following workflow triggers on the <code>repository_dispatch</code> event and uses information from the event payload to build the matrix. When a repository dispatch event is created with a payload like the one below, the matrix <code>version</code> variable will have a value of <code>[12, 14, 16]</code>. For more information about the <code>repository_dispatch</code> trigger, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows#repository_dispatch">Events that trigger workflows</a>.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-json"><span class="hljs-punctuation">{</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">&quot;event_type&quot;</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">:</span> <span class="hljs-string">&quot;test&quot;</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">,</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">&quot;client_payload&quot;</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">:</span> <span class="hljs-punctuation">{</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">&quot;versions&quot;</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">:</span> <span class="hljs-punctuation">[</span><span class="hljs-number">12</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">,</span> <span class="hljs-number">14</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">,</span> <span class="hljs-number">16</span><span class="hljs-punctuation">]</span>
  <span class="hljs-punctuation">}</span>
<span class="hljs-punctuation">}</span>
</code></pre>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">on:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">repository_dispatch:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">types:</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-string">test</span>

<span class="hljs-attr">jobs:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">example_matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">runs-on:</span> <span class="hljs-string">ubuntu-latest</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> <span class="hljs-string">${{</span> <span class="hljs-string">github.event.client_payload.versions</span> <span class="hljs-string">}}</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">steps:</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">uses:</span> <span class="hljs-string">actions/setup-node@v7</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">with:</span>
          <span class="hljs-attr">node-version:</span> <span class="hljs-string">${{</span> <span class="hljs-string">matrix.version</span> <span class="hljs-string">}}</span>
</code></pre>
<h2 id="expanding-or-adding-matrix-configurations" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#expanding-or-adding-matrix-configurations">Expanding or adding matrix configurations<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>To expand existing matrix configurations or to add new configurations, use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.matrix.include</code>. The value of <code>include</code> is a list of objects.</p>
<p>For example, consider the following matrix.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">fruit:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">apple</span>, <span class="hljs-string">pear</span>]
    <span class="hljs-attr">animal:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">cat</span>, <span class="hljs-string">dog</span>]
    <span class="hljs-attr">include:</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">color:</span> <span class="hljs-string">green</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">color:</span> <span class="hljs-string">pink</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">animal:</span> <span class="hljs-string">cat</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">fruit:</span> <span class="hljs-string">apple</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">shape:</span> <span class="hljs-string">circle</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">fruit:</span> <span class="hljs-string">banana</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">fruit:</span> <span class="hljs-string">banana</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">animal:</span> <span class="hljs-string">cat</span>
</code></pre>
<p>This will result in six jobs with the following matrix combinations.</p>
<ul>
<li><code>{fruit: apple, animal: cat, color: pink, shape: circle}</code></li>
<li><code>{fruit: apple, animal: dog, color: green, shape: circle}</code></li>
<li><code>{fruit: pear, animal: cat, color: pink}</code></li>
<li><code>{fruit: pear, animal: dog, color: green}</code></li>
<li><code>{fruit: banana}</code></li>
<li><code>{fruit: banana, animal: cat}</code></li>
</ul>
<p>Each <code>include</code> entry was applied in the following ways.</p>
<ul>
<li><code>{color: green}</code> is added to all of the original matrix combinations because it can be added without overwriting any part of the original combinations.</li>
<li><code>{color: pink, animal: cat}</code> adds <code>color:pink</code> only to the original matrix combinations that include <code>animal: cat</code>. This overwrites the <code>color: green</code> that was added by the previous <code>include</code> entry.</li>
<li><code>{fruit: apple, shape: circle}</code> adds <code>shape: circle</code> only to the original matrix combinations that include <code>fruit: apple</code>.</li>
<li><code>{fruit: banana}</code> cannot be added to any original matrix combination without overwriting a value, so it is added as an additional matrix combination.</li>
<li><code>{fruit: banana, animal: cat}</code> cannot be added to any original matrix combination without overwriting a value, so it is added as an additional matrix combination. It does not add to the <code>{fruit: banana}</code> matrix combination because that combination was not one of the original matrix combinations.</li>
</ul>
<p>For reference and example configurations, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrixinclude">Workflow syntax for GitHub Actions</a>.</p>
<h2 id="excluding-matrix-configurations" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#excluding-matrix-configurations">Excluding matrix configurations<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>To remove specific configurations defined in the matrix, use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.matrix.exclude</code>.</p>
<p>For example, the following workflow will run nine jobs: one job for each of the 12 configurations, minus the one excluded job that matches <code>{os: macos-latest, version: 12, environment: production}</code>, and the two excluded jobs that match <code>{os: windows-latest, version: 16}</code>.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">os:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">macos-latest</span>, <span class="hljs-string">windows-latest</span>]
    <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> [<span class="hljs-number">12</span>, <span class="hljs-number">14</span>, <span class="hljs-number">16</span>]
    <span class="hljs-attr">environment:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">staging</span>, <span class="hljs-string">production</span>]
    <span class="hljs-attr">exclude:</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">os:</span> <span class="hljs-string">macos-latest</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> <span class="hljs-number">12</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">environment:</span> <span class="hljs-string">production</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">os:</span> <span class="hljs-string">windows-latest</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> <span class="hljs-number">16</span>
<span class="hljs-attr">runs-on:</span> <span class="hljs-string">${{</span> <span class="hljs-string">matrix.os</span> <span class="hljs-string">}}</span>
</code></pre>
<p>For reference information, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrixexclude">Workflow syntax for GitHub Actions</a></p>
<h2 id="using-an-output-to-define-two-matrices" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#using-an-output-to-define-two-matrices">Using an output to define two matrices<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>You can use the output from one job to define matrices for multiple jobs.</p>
<p>For example, the following workflow demonstrates how to define a matrix of values in one job, use that matrix in a second jobs to produce artifacts, and then consume those artifacts in a third job. Each artifact is associated with a value from the matrix.</p>
<div class="code-example"><header class="d-flex flex-items-center flex-justify-between p-2 text-small rounded-top-1 border-top border-left border-right"><span class="flex-1">YAML</span><button type="button" aria-label="Copy YAML code to clipboard" data-clipboard="547080011" class="js-btn-copy btn btn-sm tooltipped tooltipped-nw"><svg version="1.1" width="16" height="16" viewBox="0 0 16 16" class="octicon octicon-copy" aria-hidden="true"><path d="M0 6.75C0 5.784.784 5 1.75 5h1.5a.75.75 0 0 1 0 1.5h-1.5a.25.25 0 0 0-.25.25v7.5c0 .138.112.25.25.25h7.5a.25.25 0 0 0 .25-.25v-1.5a.75.75 0 0 1 1.5 0v1.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 9.25 16h-7.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 0 14.25Z"></path><path d="M5 1.75C5 .784 5.784 0 6.75 0h7.5C15.216 0 16 .784 16 1.75v7.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 14.25 11h-7.5A1.75 1.75 0 0 1 5 9.25Zm1.75-.25a.25.25 0 0 0-.25.25v7.5c0 .138.112.25.25.25h7.5a.25.25 0 0 0 .25-.25v-7.5a.25.25 0 0 0-.25-.25Z"></path></svg></button><pre hidden="" data-clipboard="547080011">name: shared matrix
on:
  push:
  workflow_dispatch:

jobs:
  define-matrix:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest

    outputs:
      colors: ${{ steps.colors.outputs.colors }}

    steps:
      - name: Define Colors
        id: colors
        run: |
          echo &#x27;colors=[&quot;red&quot;, &quot;green&quot;, &quot;blue&quot;]&#x27; &gt;&gt; &quot;$GITHUB_OUTPUT&quot;

  produce-artifacts:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs: define-matrix
    strategy:
      matrix:
        color: ${{ fromJSON(needs.define-matrix.outputs.colors) }}

    steps:
      - name: Define Color
        env:
          color: ${{ matrix.color }}
        run: |
          echo &quot;$color&quot; &gt; color
      - name: Produce Artifact
        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v4
        with:
          name: ${{ matrix.color }}
          path: color

  consume-artifacts:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    needs:
    - define-matrix
    - produce-artifacts
    strategy:
      matrix:
        color: ${{ fromJSON(needs.define-matrix.outputs.colors) }}

    steps:
    - name: Retrieve Artifact
      uses: actions/download-artifact@v5
      with:
        name: ${{ matrix.color }}

    - name: Report Color
      run: |
        cat color
</pre></header><pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">name:</span> <span class="hljs-string">shared</span> <span class="hljs-string">matrix</span>
<span class="hljs-attr">on:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">push:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">workflow_dispatch:</span>

<span class="hljs-attr">jobs:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">define-matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">runs-on:</span> <span class="hljs-string">ubuntu-latest</span>

    <span class="hljs-attr">outputs:</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">colors:</span> <span class="hljs-string">${{</span> <span class="hljs-string">steps.colors.outputs.colors</span> <span class="hljs-string">}}</span>

    <span class="hljs-attr">steps:</span>
      <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">name:</span> <span class="hljs-string">Define</span> <span class="hljs-string">Colors</span>

<h2 id="handling-failures" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#handling-failures">Handling failures<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>To control how job failures are handled, use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.fail-fast</code> and <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.continue-on-error</code>.</p>
<p>You can use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.fail-fast</code> and <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.continue-on-error</code> together. For example, the following workflow will start four jobs. For each job, <code>continue-on-error</code> is determined by the value of <code>matrix.experimental</code>. If any of the jobs with <code>continue-on-error: false</code> fail, all jobs that are in progress or queued will be cancelled. If the job with <code>continue-on-error: true</code> fails, the other jobs will not be affected.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">jobs:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">test:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">runs-on:</span> <span class="hljs-string">ubuntu-latest</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">continue-on-error:</span> <span class="hljs-string">${{</span> <span class="hljs-string">matrix.experimental</span> <span class="hljs-string">}}</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">fail-fast:</span> <span class="hljs-literal">true</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> [<span class="hljs-number">6</span>, <span class="hljs-number">7</span>, <span class="hljs-number">8</span>]
        <span class="hljs-attr">experimental:</span> [<span class="hljs-literal">false</span>]
        <span class="hljs-attr">include:</span>
          <span class="hljs-bullet">-</span> <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> <span class="hljs-number">9</span>
            <span class="hljs-attr">experimental:</span> <span class="hljs-literal">true</span>
</code></pre>
<p>For reference information see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategyfail-fast"><code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.fail-fast</code></a> and <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idcontinue-on-error"><code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.continue-on-error</code></a>.</p>
<h2 id="defining-the-maximum-number-of-concurrent-jobs" tabindex="-1"><a class="heading-link" href="#defining-the-maximum-number-of-concurrent-jobs">Defining the maximum number of concurrent jobs<span class="heading-link-symbol" aria-hidden="true"></span></a></h2>
<p>To set the maximum number of jobs that can run simultaneously when using a <code>matrix</code> job strategy, use <code>jobs.&lt;job_id&gt;.strategy.max-parallel</code>.</p>
<p>For example, the following workflow will run a maximum of two jobs at a time, even if there are runners available to run all six jobs at once.</p>
<pre><code class="hljs language-yaml"><span class="hljs-attr">jobs:</span>
  <span class="hljs-attr">example_matrix:</span>
    <span class="hljs-attr">strategy:</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">max-parallel:</span> <span class="hljs-number">2</span>
      <span class="hljs-attr">matrix:</span>
        <span class="hljs-attr">version:</span> [<span class="hljs-number">10</span>, <span class="hljs-number">12</span>, <span class="hljs-number">14</span>]
        <span class="hljs-attr">os:</span> [<span class="hljs-string">ubuntu-latest</span>, <span class="hljs-string">windows-latest</span>]
</code></pre>
<p>For reference information, see <a href="/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymax-parallel">Workflow syntax for GitHub Actions</a>.</p></div></div></div></div></div></main><div aria-hidden="true" class="FooterDivider_footerDivider__6q_kx"></div><footer class="Primer_Brand__MinimalFooter-module__MinimalFooter___QucKd aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eA matrix strategy lets you use variables in a single job definition to automatically create multiple job runs that are based on the combinations of the variables. For example, you can use a matrix strategy to test your code in multiple versions of a language or on multiple operating systems.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"adding-a-matrix-strategy-to-your-workflow-job\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#adding-a-matrix-strategy-to-your-workflow-job\"\u003eAdding a matrix strategy to your workflow job\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eUse \u003ccode\u003ejobs.\u0026#x3C;job_id\u003e.strategy.matrix\u003c/code\u003e to define a matrix of different job configurations. Within your matrix, define one or more variables followed by an array of values. For example, the following matrix has a variable called \u003ccode\u003eversion\u003c/code\u003e with the value \u003ccode\u003e[10, 12, 14]\u003c/code\u003e and a variable called \u003ccode\u003eos\u003c/code\u003e with the value \u003ccode\u003e[ubuntu-latest, windows-latest]\u003c/code\u003e:\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-yaml\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ejobs:\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eexample_matrix:\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003estrategy:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ematrix:\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eversion:\u003c/span\u003e [\u003cspan class=\"hljs-number\"\u003e10\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"hljs-number\"\u003e12\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"hljs-number\"\u003e14\u003c/span\u003e]\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eos:\u003c/span\u003e [\u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eubuntu-latest\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ewindows-latest\u003c/span\u003e]\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eA job will run for each possible combination of the variables. In this example, the workflow will run six jobs, one for each combination of the \u003ccode\u003eos\u003c/code\u003e and \u003ccode\u003eversion\u003c/code\u003e variables.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThe above matrix will create the jobs in the following order.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 10, os: ubuntu-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 10, os: windows-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 12, os: ubuntu-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 12, os: windows-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 14, os: ubuntu-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{version: 14, os: windows-latest}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor reference information and examples, see \u003ca href=\"/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrix\"\u003eWorkflow syntax for GitHub Actions\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"using-contexts-to-create-matrices\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#using-contexts-to-create-matrices\"\u003eUsing contexts to create matrices\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTo create matrices with information about workflow runs, variables, runner environments, jobs, and steps, access contexts using the \u003ccode\u003e${{ \u0026#x3C;context\u003e }}\u003c/code\u003e expression syntax. For more information about contexts, see \u003ca href=\"/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/contexts\"\u003eContexts reference\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor example, the following workflow triggers on the \u003ccode\u003erepository_dispatch\u003c/code\u003e event and uses information from the event payload to build the matrix. When a repository dispatch event is created with a payload like the one below, the matrix \u003ccode\u003eversion\u003c/code\u003e variable will have a value of \u003ccode\u003e[12, 14, 16]\u003c/code\u003e. For more information about the \u003ccode\u003erepository_dispatch\u003c/code\u003e trigger, see \u003ca href=\"/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/events-that-trigger-workflows#repository_dispatch\"\u003eEvents that trigger workflows\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-json\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-punctuation\"\u003e{\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003e\"event_type\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-punctuation\"\u003e:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003e\"test\"\u003c/span\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-punctuation\"\u003e,\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-punctuation\"\u003e}\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"hljs-punctuation\"\u003e}\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-yaml\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eon:\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003erepository_dispatch:\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003etypes:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003etest\u003c/span\u003e\n\n\u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ejobs:\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eexample_matrix:\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eruns-on:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eubuntu-latest\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003estrategy:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ematrix:\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eversion:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003e${{\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003egithub.event.client_payload.versions\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003e}}\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003esteps:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003euses:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eactions/setup-node@v7\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ewith:\u003c/span\u003e\n          \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003enode-version:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003e${{\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ematrix.version\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003e}}\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"expanding-or-adding-matrix-configurations\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#expanding-or-adding-matrix-configurations\"\u003eExpanding or adding matrix configurations\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTo expand existing matrix configurations or to add new configurations, use \u003ccode\u003ejobs.\u0026#x3C;job_id\u003e.strategy.matrix.include\u003c/code\u003e. The value of \u003ccode\u003einclude\u003c/code\u003e is a list of objects.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor example, consider the following matrix.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-yaml\"\u003e\u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003estrategy:\u003c/span\u003e\n  \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ematrix:\u003c/span\u003e\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003efruit:\u003c/span\u003e [\u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eapple\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003epear\u003c/span\u003e]\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eanimal:\u003c/span\u003e [\u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ecat\u003c/span\u003e, \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003edog\u003c/span\u003e]\n    \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003einclude:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ecolor:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003egreen\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003ecolor:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003epink\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eanimal:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ecat\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003efruit:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eapple\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eshape:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ecircle\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003efruit:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ebanana\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003efruit:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ebanana\u003c/span\u003e\n        \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eanimal:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003ecat\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/pre\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eThis will result in six jobs with the following matrix combinations.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: apple, animal: cat, color: pink, shape: circle}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: apple, animal: dog, color: green, shape: circle}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: pear, animal: cat, color: pink}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: pear, animal: dog, color: green}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: banana}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: banana, animal: cat}\u003c/code\u003e\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eEach \u003ccode\u003einclude\u003c/code\u003e entry was applied in the following ways.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cul\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{color: green}\u003c/code\u003e is added to all of the original matrix combinations because it can be added without overwriting any part of the original combinations.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{color: pink, animal: cat}\u003c/code\u003e adds \u003ccode\u003ecolor:pink\u003c/code\u003e only to the original matrix combinations that include \u003ccode\u003eanimal: cat\u003c/code\u003e. This overwrites the \u003ccode\u003ecolor: green\u003c/code\u003e that was added by the previous \u003ccode\u003einclude\u003c/code\u003e entry.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: apple, shape: circle}\u003c/code\u003e adds \u003ccode\u003eshape: circle\u003c/code\u003e only to the original matrix combinations that include \u003ccode\u003efruit: apple\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: banana}\u003c/code\u003e cannot be added to any original matrix combination without overwriting a value, so it is added as an additional matrix combination.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003cli\u003e\u003ccode\u003e{fruit: banana, animal: cat}\u003c/code\u003e cannot be added to any original matrix combination without overwriting a value, so it is added as an additional matrix combination. It does not add to the \u003ccode\u003e{fruit: banana}\u003c/code\u003e matrix combination because that combination was not one of the original matrix combinations.\u003c/li\u003e\n\u003c/ul\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eFor reference and example configurations, see \u003ca href=\"/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrixinclude\"\u003eWorkflow syntax for GitHub Actions\u003c/a\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"excluding-matrix-configurations\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#excluding-matrix-configurations\"\u003eExcluding matrix configurations\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eTo remove specific configurations defined in the matrix, use \u003ccode\u003e{os: windows-latest, version: 16}\u003c/code\u003e.\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003cpre\u003e\u003ccode class=\"hljs language-yaml\"\u003e\u003cspan \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eexclude:\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eos:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan \u003cspan class=\"hljs-attr\"\u003eenvironment:\u003c/span\u003e \u003cspan class=\"hljs-string\"\u003eproduction\u003c/span\u003e\n      \u003cspan class=\"hljs-bullet\"\u003e-\u003c/span\u003e href=\"/en/actions/reference/workflows-and-actions/workflow-syntax#jobsjob_idstrategymatrixexclude\"\u003eWorkflow syntax for GitHub Actions\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/p\u003e\n\u003ch2 id=\"using-an-output-to-define-two-matrices\" tabindex=\"-1\"\u003e\u003ca class=\"heading-link\" href=\"#using-an-output-to-define-two-matrices\"\u003eUsing an output to define two matrices\u003cspan class=\"heading-link-symbol\" aria-hidden=\"true\"\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\u003c/a\u003e\u003c/h2\u003e\n\u003cp\u003eYou can use the output from one job to define matrices for multiple data-clipboard=\"547080011\"\u003ename: shared matrix\non:\n  push:\n  workflow_dispatch:\n\njobs:\n  define-matrix:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n\n    outputs:\n      colors: ${{ \"$GITHUB_OUTPUT\"\n\n  produce-artifacts:\n    runs-on: ubuntu-latest\n    needs: define-matrix\n    strategy:\n      matrix:\n        color: ${{ fromJSON(needs.define-matrix.outputs.colors) }}\n\n
